# envoy-rust Roadmap

## Schema

Each phase row has the columns:

- **id** — stable numeric identifier (`00`, `01`, …; sub-phases `NN.1`, `NN.2`).
- **title** — one-line human-readable name.
- **depends-on** — space-separated phase ids that must be `done` before this one can enter `in-progress`.
- **status** — one of `planned | in-progress | blocked | done`.
- **sub-phases** — if the phase has been split (§6 of `BOOTSTRAP_PROMPT.md`), list the child ids here.
- **summary** — the differential surface this phase is expected to light up when it lands.

Rules:

- This file is **append-only history** — rows are never deleted. Only the `status` and `sub-phases` columns are mutated.
- `STATE.md` names the single active phase. A phase enters `in-progress` only when `STATE.md` points at it.
- When a phase is split, its own `status` becomes `in-progress` while its sub-phases land. The parent flips to `done` only after all sub-phases are `done`.

---

## Feature Families — phases 09 and onward (headings only)

These are seeded as headings only. Each family becomes one or more concrete phase rows when it enters `in-progress`, at which point it is brainstormed and split (§6) as reality demands. Do **not** expand them into per-phase rows prematurely.

### HTTP filters family

Header manipulation, cors, compression, fault, local+global rate limit, jwt_authn, rbac, ext_authz, ext_proc, oauth2, csrf, buffer, lua, wasm, adaptive concurrency, admission control, bandwidth limit.

| 25 | envoy.filters.http.buffer (per-route typed_per_filter_config — the THIRD consumer) + H1 request-body forwarding + fixture 0033 | 04 05 07 23 | done | 25.1, 25.2 | (parent — **SPLIT into `25.1` + `25.2` per ADR-0064** at the state-2 PLAN-write; §6.2 verified + reconciled by **ADR-0063** [413 body `Payload Too Large` 17B via the existing decorate helpers; reject strictly `> max_request_bytes`; NO buffer-scoped stats → SPEC §2.1 row DROPPED, the over-limit 413 reflected only in the generic HCM `downstream_rq_too_large`; `Buffer`/`BufferPerRoute` wire shapes confirmed as projected; absent-`buffer` per-route config accepted-inert by Envoy → reuse the generic `PerRouteConfigForAbsentFilter` validator verbatim]; parent flips `done` only after both sub-phases are `done`. SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/25-http-filter-buffer/`, scope locked by ADR-0062 at the state-1 brainstorm) target: fixture 0033-http-filter-buffer green on an H1 listener proxying to a real `http1-echo-server` cluster (all 33 Docker-gated fixtures 0001–0033 green simultaneously); the SEVENTH concrete HTTP-filter-family phase (after 09 local_ratelimit / 10 rbac / 11 fault / 22 jwt_authn / 23 cors / 24 csrf), the THIRD consumer of the phase-23 per-route `typed_per_filter_config` infrastructure (after cors+csrf), AND the project's FIRST body-dependent HTTP filter (the easy header-only filter vein is exhausted — all 7 shipped filters are header-only decode-side decisions; the framework is headers-only with no streaming decode_data hook). **Two-part scope (§6.1 split LIKELY):** Part A (projected 25.1) closes the pre-existing H1 request-body-forwarding gap — today the H1 router forwards an always-empty body upstream (`hcm.rs:356`) and drains+discards the downstream Content-Length body (`:678-697`), so envoy-rust cannot proxy an H1 POST body at all; Part A reads the body into a `Bytes`, populates `FilterRequest.body`, and forwards it upstream (regression-equivalence: all 32 existing fixtures green; the 07.1/12.1/14.1/23.1 foundation-slice pattern; chunked/streaming bodies remain a non-goal). Part B (projected 25.2) adds the `BufferFilter` (ninth `HttpFilterInstance` variant; decode-side-only `max_request_bytes`/413 guard reusing the existing H1/H2 filter-synth helpers; per-route `BufferPerRoute` disable/limit-override threaded via the existing phase-23 `apply_route_config` hook — NO HCM change) + `Buffer { max_request_bytes }` chain config + `BufferPerRoute` as the third `PerFilterConfig` variant + reuse of the `PerRouteConfigForAbsentFilter` validator + conditional `http.<stat_prefix>.buffer.*` stats (namespace §6.2-verified) + fixture 0033 (5 deterministic header+body probes: within-limit→200+echoed-body / over-limit→413 / per-route-disabled→200 / per-route-lowered-limit→413 / GET-no-body→200) + an in-process backstop + a new `parse_bootstrap` fuzz seed (no new fuzz target). H2 already buffers+forwards request bodies so the filter works on H2 for free (no H2 fixture required this phase). Minimum-viable scope per ADR-0062 (defers chunked/streaming bodies, the generic streaming decode_data framework hook, encode-side buffering, vhost-level BufferPerRoute + the route>vhost cascade, typed_per_filter_config for filters OTHER than cors/csrf/buffer, gRPC/trailers); scope locked by ADR-0062 at the state-1 brainstorm **(done - parent CLOSED at the 25.2 state-6 close-out 2026-06-15: both sub-phases `25.1` + `25.2` `done`; all 33 Docker-gated fixtures `0001`-`0033` green simultaneously; state-4 §7.5 gate GREEN [AUTHORITATIVE Linux CI `27510477930` at `fde99b984`]; state-5 `REVIEW.md` APPROVED 0 Critical / 0 Important / 4 Minor; artifacts at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/25.2-http-filter-buffer/`, parent SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/25-http-filter-buffer/`)** |
| 25.1 | H1 request-body forwarding (Content-Length-delimited) — read the downstream body into a `Bytes` before the filter pipeline, expose it as `FilterRequest.body`, forward it upstream (no new fixture) | 04 07 | done | — | Part A of parent `25` (the ADR-0064 split). The FOUNDATION SLICE closing the pre-existing H1 request-body-forwarding gap: today the H1 router forwards an always-empty body upstream (`crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:356`) and drains-and-discards the downstream Content-Length body (`:678-697`), so envoy-rust cannot proxy an H1 POST body at all (a gap since phase 04.3). (state-0/1 brainstorm complete 2026-07-09: `SPEC.md` authored at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/67-network-filter-rbac/`; pick + scope locked by **ADR-0128**; **ADR-0129 RESERVED** for the projected §6.1 split. State-0 recon drove the pinned `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0` and established, verbatim: `[rbac, echo]` validates so rbac is NON-TERMINAL; `[rbac]` alone is REJECTED with `non-terminal filter named envoy.filters.network.rbac ... is the last filter in a network filter chain` — the bilateral dual of the terminal rule phase 66 landed, and a gap phase 66 could not have found because envoy-rust then had no non-terminal filter. On DENY, Envoy writes ZERO bytes and closes with a clean EOF (no RST) and the client's already-sent bytes are discarded; on ALLOW the payload round-trips through the terminal `echo`. Counters are `<stat_prefix>.rbac.allowed` / `.denied` / `.shadow_allowed` / `.shadow_denied`; `stat_prefix` is REQUIRED and `rules` is OPTIONAL — and with `rules` omitted the filter is INERT, allowing the connection while incrementing NEITHER counter (not `default action ALLOW, counted`). L4 RBAC accepts `direct_remote_ip` / `remote_ip` / `source_ip` / `destination_port` / `destination_ip`, REJECTS `header` matchers at config load, and ACCEPTS `url_path` though it can never match at L4. envoy-rust already models `RbacConfig` / `Rules` / `Policy` / `Permission` / `Principal` from the HTTP RBAC filter, but with HTTP-shaped arms only, so reuse is PARTIAL not free; and `crates/envoy-filter/src/rbac.rs` is the HTTP filter — the same do-not-conflate hazard `direct_response` had. The differential harness has `expected_stats` on only three HTTP `Driver` variants and NONE of the four raw-TCP ones, so a DENY fixture asserting only `both returned zero bytes` would pass VACUOUSLY even if RBAC were never implemented; extending the raw-TCP driver family with `expected_stats` is therefore an in-scope HARD REQUIREMENT. The iteration protocol is scoped to connection-establishment only (`on_new_connection` returning Continue or StopIteration) because RBAC never reads a downstream byte; `on_data` iteration is deferred as CF-67-3 to the first payload-parsing network filter, per §6.3's no-incomplete-stubs rule. CONSUMES CF-66-2 + M66-3 (the `JoinSet` non-reaping and unbounded per-connection drain shared verbatim by `echo.rs` and `direct_response.rs` — this phase restructures BOTH accept loops, the repair unit the phase-66 review demanded) + M66-4. CLOSES M66-5 on measured evidence with NO code change: an empty `filters: []` network chain is ACCEPTED by upstream Envoy, so envoy-rust's acceptance is PARITY not a divergence (the reviewer's intuition that Envoy rejects it was WRONG, vindicating the D-3.3 discipline of asserting only what was measured), and `direct_response` with `response: {}` is REJECTED by both proxies. OPENS CF-67-1 (`shadow_rules`), CF-67-2 (`Action::LOG`), CF-67-3 (`on_data` iteration), CF-67-4 (the L4 matcher-arm posture). NO new crate / dependency / fuzz target (§7.4 gate (d) satisfied by the pre-existing `parse_bootstrap` target — network rbac parses nothing; a corpus seed is added instead). Chosen over `sni_cluster` (needs a `tls_inspector` LISTENER filter, a subsystem envoy-rust wholly lacks — strictly heavier for the same CF-66-2 payoff), `redis_proxy` / `thrift_proxy` (terminal, so they force nothing), `mongo_proxy` / `zookeeper_proxy` / `kafka_broker` (also force `on_data` buffering), xDS CDS/LDS hot-reload, the non-deterministic LB policies, `DC`, and the doc-only carry-forward leaves. Estimated ~1740 net LoC / ~16-20 tasks, so the §6.1 split is projected to FIRE at the state-2 PLAN-write: `67.1` = protocol + chain-termination rule + rbac with `any`-only matchers + fixtures + the harness `expected_stats` extension; `67.2` = the connection-level matcher arms + `CidrRange` + the CF-67-4 rejections. **§6.1 single-phase confirmed, ADR-0075 UNFIRED** [~9 tasks / ~900–1100 LoC — lighter than projected since validation collapsed + no stat task]; the state-3 implementation is the next session [`superpowers:subagent-driven-development`]) the THIRD concrete Load-balancing-family row, after `RING_HASH` 28 + `MAGLEV` 29 — original brainstorm scope below: the THIRD concrete Load-balancing-family row, after `RING_HASH` 28 + `MAGLEV` 29. **`subset LB` chosen as the next DETERMINISTIC, byte-exact-differentiable LB step** — unlike the remaining LB policies it needs NEITHER a contract-relaxation ADR (`least_request`/`random` are non-deterministic P2C/random) NOR active-HC health state (`priority`/`panic-thresholds`/`locality-weighted` cannot be exercised differentially without HC/outlier — all deferred). subset LB is **NOT a `LbPolicy` value**: it is an orthogonal layer that, BEFORE the cluster's existing `lb_policy` picks, NARROWS the candidate endpoints to those whose `envoy.lb` metadata is a SUPERSET of the matched route's `metadata_match`, then runs the inner `lb_policy` (MVP ROUND_ROBIN) within the subset. **Differential is LOCALLY observable** (a normal request/response, no file-watch/reload trigger — like 28/29). D-items: endpoint `metadata: Option<Metadata>` (the `envoy.lb` namespace) on `LbEndpoint`; `Cluster.lb_subset_config: Option<LbSubsetConfig>` (`subset_selectors` [keys], `fallback_policy` [NO_FALLBACK default / ANY_ENDPOINT / DEFAULT_SUBSET], `default_subset`); route `RouteAction.metadata_match: Option<Metadata>`; a new `crates/envoy-cluster/src/subset.rs` (build the subset index over endpoints per selector; lookup by the route `metadata_match` → the matching subset; superset-match); the route `metadata_match` threaded to `pick()`/`pick_endpoint()` (the phase-28 `Option<u64>` hash-key threading is the template; a cluster with no `lb_subset_config` ignores it → no-op regression); the three `fallback_policy` dispositions (NO_FALLBACK → 503 no-healthy-upstream; ANY_ENDPOINT → all hosts; DEFAULT_SUBSET → the default subset); fixture 0038 (one subset cluster, two distinguishable backends [the `--body-marker` seed] with distinct `envoy.lb` metadata [`stage: prod` / `stage: canary`], routes with `metadata_match` asserting same-route→same-backend bilaterally + a fallback probe; clone of fixture-0037's shape incl. `discover_host_lan_ip`/`{{BACKEND_IP}}`) + an in-process backstop + a BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT "LB selection" subset row + a `parse_bootstrap` fuzz seed. Load-bearing invariant: all 37 pre-existing fixtures `0001`–`0037` stay green (the subset narrowing is a no-op when `lb_subset_config` is absent — regression-equivalence for the round-robin AND RING_HASH/MAGLEV paths). minimum-viable scope per ADR-0073 (defers least_request/random; priority/panic-thresholds/locality-weighted LB; subset+consistent-hash-inner-LB; multiple overlapping selectors / per-selector fallback / single_host_per_subset / list_as_any / metadata_fallback_policy / scale_locality_weight; subset+EDS-hot-reload; subset+HC/OD differential; CDS/LDS hot-reload; the gRPC/ADS transport [still ADR-0014/H2-trailers-blocked]). The core differential risk (envoy-rust must replicate Envoy's EXACT subset selection + fallback to select the SAME backend) is de-risked by the state-2 §6.2 LOCAL reconnaissance (record `metadata_match`→backend mappings + the fallback dispositions + the wire shape against `envoyproxy/envoy:v1.33.0`); **ADR-0074 reserved** for the §6.2 reconciliation, **ADR-0075 reserved** for the §6.1 split (projected NOT to fire but the closest call since phase 22 — ~1100–1500 LoC / ~12–15 tasks; the seam is 30.1 config+engine / 30.2 pick-threading+fallback+fixture+close). Weighs the open M29-1/M29-2 carry-forwards (the shared `Http1HashSweep` driver's RING_HASH-worded diagnostics — fold if fixture 0038 reuses that driver). The state-2 PLAN-write is the next session [`superpowers:writing-plans`]) |

reuses `Driver::Http1KeepAlive` + the 16.x stateful backend (no new Driver, no new helper); minimum-viable scope per ADR-0046 (defers the `max_pending_requests > 0` pending queue [owner: a future pool-hardening + pending-queue phase], `retry_budget` percent budgets, `max_connection_pools`, multi-priority HIGH thresholds, concurrency-regime differential fixtures, `per_try_timeout`); after this phase the Upstream-robustness family is complete in minimum-viable form |

### HTTP/3 + QUIC family

quinn transport, downstream H3 listener, upstream H3 cluster, `h3spec` gate.

### gRPC family

gRPC bridge, gRPC-Web, gRPC-JSON transcoding, interop conformance.

### xDS / dynamic config family

ADS, delta xDS, LDS, CDS, RDS, EDS, SDS, RTDS, reconnection, initial-fetch timeout.

Lands the xDS family's repeatedly-deferred PRIME FOLLOW-UP — hot reload — at its minimum-viable increment: a running HCM whose route table is RDS-supplied picks up an edited RDS file WITHOUT a restart, re-routing live traffic, observable via the per-HCM `http.<stat_prefix>.rds.<route_config_name>.{update_attempt,update_success,config_reload}` counters advancing PAST their initial-load values + `RoutesConfigDump` reflecting the new version. **RDS first because its live mutation is the LEAST invasive** — the route table is already `route_config: Arc<RouteConfiguration>` (`crates/envoy-http1/src/hcm.rs:122`) and route matching is per-request stateless, so hot-reload = an atomic pointer swap (NO drain / socket churn / pool/health/outlier lifecycle — the reasons CDS/LDS/EDS hot-reload defer). D1 migrate `HCMConfig.route_config` Arc → a shared swappable handle (dep-free `RwLock<Arc<…>>`/`tokio::sync::watch`, NO new `arc-swap` dep); D2 an `RdsWatcher` — the FIFTH periodic-background primitive (poll mtime, `CancellationToken` discipline, mirroring `envoy-health::Scheduler` + the 13.x/14.2 sweepers); D3 reload pipeline (re-parse via existing `rds.rs` → re-validate route→cluster refs against the immutable live cluster set → atomic swap; tick `rds.*` per reload; **warm-reject keeping the last-good table on a bad reload — the one ADR-0049 startup-all-fatal carve-out**); D4 per-reload counter increments; D5 `RoutesConfigDump` version/`last_updated` update read through the swappable handle; D6 the one new harness capability (mid-test fixture-file rewrite + settle-then-probe, the 12.2 wait-for-convergence pattern); D7 fixture 0034 (two distinguishable real `http1-echo-server` clusters; three-phase pre-reload→reload→post-reload + a bad-reload probe; **Linux-CI-authoritative per ADR-0049** — the reload trigger is unobservable on macOS Docker virtiofs, the phase-21 watching-class precedent); D8 in-process backstop (deterministic local complement via `tokio::time`, drives the negative paths + in-flight isolation) + BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT extensions. **Minimum-viable hot-reload adds ZERO new config schema** (Envoy's file xDS is always-watching; envoy-rust watches every `rds`-configured HCM) UNLESS §6.2 reveals Envoy needs `watched_directory` for the chosen file-change operation. Regression witness: the existing fixture `0028-xds-file-based-rds` gains an IDLE watcher (file never edited mid-test → counters/data-plane unchanged → 0028 stays green; the load-bearing proof the handle migration is behavior-preserving). minimum-viable scope per ADR-0065 (defers CDS/LDS/EDS hot-reload [harder live mutations, layer onto this watcher+swap primitive], inotify-exactness/`watched_directory`, cluster warming, configurable poll interval, scoped_routes/SRDS/VHDS hot-reload, SDS/RTDS, the gRPC/ADS transport + the ADR-0014 protos supersession, delta xDS, `initial_fetch_timeout`); SPEC at `docs/envoy-rust/phases/26-xds-rds-hot-reload/`, scope locked by ADR-0065 at the state-1 brainstorm; ADR-0066 reserved for the §6.2 reconciliation (PLAUSIBLE — `watched_directory`/reload-counter/bad-reload/config_dump shapes), ADR-0067 for the §6.1 split (projected NOT to fire; ~1200–1600 LoC / ~10–13 tasks) |

Lands the FIRST of the three ADR-0065-deferred hot-reload layers (CDS/LDS/EDS), **EDS chosen as the LEAST invasive** — an EDS reload mutates ONLY a cluster's endpoint `Vec<SocketAddr>` (`crates/envoy-cluster/src/cluster.rs:80`, read every request by the round-robin LB `pick()` `:274` — per-request stateless, the closest analog to phase-26's stateless route-table swap); the cluster lifecycle (pools/health/outlier/budgets/listeners) stays immutable. A running cluster whose endpoint set is EDS-supplied picks up an edited EDS file WITHOUT a restart, re-targeting live traffic, observable via the per-cluster `cluster.<name>.{update_attempt,update_success}` counters advancing PAST their initial-load values (1/1 → 2/2) + `EndpointsConfigDump` reflecting the new assignment. **Reuses phase-26's infrastructure wholesale** — the periodic poll-watch primitive (`crates/envoy-http1/src/rds_watcher.rs`, generalized — the M19-1/M20/M21 + now-two-watchers `XdsFileWatcher` factoring), the atomic-swap-handle pattern, the warm-reject reload pipeline, and the mid-test atomic-rename rewrite + settle-then-probe harness capability — so NO new crate, NO new dep, NO new reload/settle harness machinery. D1 migrate `Cluster.endpoints` Vec → a shared swappable handle (dep-free `RwLock<Arc<…>>`/`tokio::sync::watch`; LB reads through it; cursor-bounds-safe on a shrinking set); D2 the EDS file watcher (generalize the phase-26 watcher / a sibling, likely relocated to a shared crate since EDS concerns `envoy-cluster`); D3 reload pipeline (re-parse via existing `eds.rs` → re-validate the assignment → atomic swap; tick `cluster.<name>.update_*` per reload; warm-reject keeping the last-good set — reusing the phase-26 §5.5 carve-out); D4 per-reload counter increments; D5 `EndpointsConfigDump` `last_updated`/assignment read through the swappable handle; D6 the one new harness capability (a SECOND distinguishable backend — an intra-cluster endpoint swap is invisible through one echo backend [`membership_total` not emitted `cluster.rs:963`, `membership_healthy` HC-gated]; the minimal seed of the deferred LB distinguishable-backend harness); D7 fixture 0035 (two distinguishable real `http1-echo-server` backends; one `type: EDS` cluster `eds_backend` with NO HC/NO OD; three-phase pre-reload→reload→post-reload + a bad-reload probe; **native-Linux-CI-authoritative per ADR-0049/0066** — the reload trigger is unobservable under virtiofs); D8 in-process backstop (negative paths + in-flight isolation + cursor-bounds) + BEHAVIOR_CONTRACT extensions. **Minimum-viable scope = PLAIN clusters only (no active HC / no outlier detection)** — the endpoint-index-aligned `endpoint_health`/`outlier_detection` arrays + per-`(cluster,endpoint)` health-probe tasks would need spawn/drain/rebuild on a count-changing reload (the same per-endpoint lifecycle churn that defers CDS), so the EDS+HC/OD aligned-array rebuild defers. Regression witness: the existing fixture `0029-xds-file-based-eds` gains an IDLE endpoint-watcher (file never edited mid-test → counters/data-plane unchanged → 0029 stays green; the load-bearing proof the endpoint-handle migration is behavior-preserving). minimum-viable scope per ADR-0067 (defers CDS/LDS hot-reload [heavier live mutations], the EDS+HC/OD aligned-array rebuild, `LbEndpoint` weight/health-status/locality/priority reload, cluster warming, configurable poll interval, SDS/RTDS, the gRPC/ADS transport + the ADR-0014 protos supersession, delta xDS, `initial_fetch_timeout`); **ADR-0068 FIRED** at the state-2 PLAN-write (the §6.2 reconciliation — observability = second backend; empty-assignment MIRRORS apply-empty→503; config_dump no `last_updated`; `update_rejected` emitted; EDS+HC/OD = no watcher), **ADR-0069 UNFIRED** (the §6.1 split did NOT fire — single phase; ~900–1200 LoC / 9 tasks) |

gRPC ALS, OTLP access log, OTel/Zipkin/Jaeger/Datadog/XRay tracing, stats sinks, tap filter.
